> AGENT

Short answer: partially.

Today, if you select a clip in the video editor and send a message to the agent, the UI attaches `clipId`, `url`, `mediaType`, and `generationId` from that selection, but not structured timeline fields like `track`, `at`, `duration`, `end`, `shotId`, or pinned-group membership. See [useSelectedMediaClips.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSelectedMediaClips.ts#L29), [AgentChat.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.tsx#L299), and [useAgentSession.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useAgentSession.ts#L271).

What happens after that is the server loads the full timeline, builds a text summary of every clip with `id`, `track`, `at`, and `duration`, and matches the selected `clip_id` back against that text. So the model can infer “this selected clip is on V1 at 12.0s for 5s, so after it means 17.0s”, but that is inference from prompt text, not first-class structured context. See [timeline.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/timeline.ts#L57), [prompts.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/prompts.ts#L40), and [loop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/loop.ts#L379).

For shot awareness, there are two different meanings:

- Same shot in the generations database: yes, partially. The agent resolves selected clip generation IDs to a shared shot, loads that shot’s travel settings/reference, and `create_task` will reuse that shot when it can. `duplicate_generation` also duplicates into a shot with the same `timeline_frame`. See [loop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/loop.ts#L381), [create-task.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/create-task.ts#L230), and [duplicate-generation.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/duplicate-generation.ts#L26).
- Same shot as a timeline group: not reliably. The editor does have `pinnedShotGroups` and can detect whether a clip is inside one, but that membership is not sent to the agent, and the timeline summary does not mention it. See [types/index.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts#L171) and [pinned-group-projection.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts#L20).

So for your example, “make the image after this be an edit of this image with a more aerial view”:

- The agent can probably figure out which clip “this” is and where “after this” is.
- It can create an edit/variant from the selected image. The prompt even tells it to do `duplicate_generation` -> optional `create_task(... based_on ...)` -> `add-media` after the source clip. See [prompts.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/prompts.ts#L154).
- But it is not a clean end-to-end primitive today, because `create_task` only queues an async job and returns a task id, not a finished asset ready to insert. See [generation.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/generation.ts#L323).
- `add-media` just inserts a free clip on a track at a time. It does not update shot-group membership. See [timeline.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/tools/timeline.ts#L373).

One nuance: if the selected clip lives inside an image-mode pinned shot group, the editor has a separate sync path that can rebuild that group from shot contents. That means a new generation added to the same shot may show up through shot sync, but that is indirect and not the same as “insert the result immediately after the selected clip in the same group.” See [TimelineEditor.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx#L547) and [usePinnedShotGroups.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts#L167).

If you want this flow to be solid, the missing piece is a structured selection context and a dedicated tool, not more prompting. I would add something like `selected_context: { clip_id, track_id, at, duration, end, shot_id, pinned_group: { shot_id, track_id, clip_ids } }` plus a tool like `create_variant_after_clip(...)` that handles generate, wait, and place as one operation.

> AGENT

No. Today a timeline shot is not attached to the agent as a special “shot” object.

Current behavior:
- The strip above the shot group single-clicks to open the shot menu, not to select the shot. See [TimelineCanvas.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx#L1062).
- That same strip double-clicks to select all `clipIds` in the group. See [TimelineCanvas.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx#L1073).
- `onSelectClips` goes through the normal multi-select path, so the editor selection becomes “these clip IDs are selected”, not “this shot is selected”. See [TimelineEditor.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx#L872) and [useMultiSelect.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useMultiSelect.ts#L106).

What the agent gets:
- When you send to the agent, it serializes the current selection as a list of clip attachments with `clip_id`, `url`, `media_type`, and optional `generation_id`. No `shotId`, no grouped-shot payload. See [AgentChat.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.tsx#L303) and [useAgentSession.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useAgentSession.ts#L275).
- So if you double-click a shot and then run the agent, it will send the whole shot only as multiple individual selected clips, not as one special shot attachment.

There is shot metadata in the editor:
- Pinned shot groups do exist as `shotId + trackId + clipIds`. See [types/index.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts#L171).
- But that grouped context is not currently included in the agent payload.

So your desired behavior:
- “Left click the part above the shot should select the shot”: not implemented today.
- “Agent should know I selected a whole shot”: only indirectly if all member clips are selected; it still sees them as clips, not as a shot.

If you want, I can change this so:
1. single-click on the shot strip selects the whole shot,
2. right-click opens the menu,
3. agent payload includes structured shot context like `shot_id`, `track_id`, and `clip_ids`.

> DEVELOPER

So can you summarise as a whole all the stuff we need to do based on our conversation? Keep it succint

> AGENT

We need to make shot selection and agent context first-class instead of inferred.

- Change the shot strip behavior so single-click selects the whole shot group, not the menu.
- Keep menu access on right-click, and optionally double-click for navigate/open if needed.
- When a shot is selected, store it as structured selection state, not just a set of clip IDs.
- Send structured shot context to the agent: `shot_id`, `track_id`, ordered `clip_ids`, start/end, and the anchor clip if there is one.
- Also send structured selected clip timeline context: `track`, `at`, `duration`, `end`, and pinned-group membership.
- Update the agent prompt/tools so “after this clip” and “in the same shot” use structured fields, not text matching against the timeline summary.
- Add a dedicated workflow/tool for “duplicate/edit/place after selected clip” so it can generate, wait for completion, and insert automatically.
- Add placement logic for shot groups so new results can be inserted into the same pinned shot group, not just added as a free clip.
- Keep shot DB context and timeline shot-group context separate but linked, so “same shot” means the right thing in both systems.
- Add tests for single-shot selection, agent payload shape, “whole shot selected” behavior, and duplicate/edit/place-after flow.

> AGENT

`megaplan` initialized as `implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052`, but the CLI `plan` worker hung before writing artifacts even after rerouting from Codex to Claude. I’m giving you the equivalent implementation megaplan directly.

**Megaplan**
1. Define the selection model.
Clarify three distinct selection types in the editor: single clip, multi-clip, and shot-group. Add a first-class shot selection shape that carries `shotId`, `trackId`, ordered `clipIds`, anchor clip, start, end, and mode.

2. Fix timeline shot-group UX.
Change the shot label strip so single-click selects the whole shot, right-click opens the menu, and double-click does only an intentional secondary action if we still want one. Make the visual selected state obvious at the group level.

3. Thread structured selection through the frontend.
Update selection hooks, timeline context, and agent-chat attachment building so the agent receives structured shot and clip timeline metadata, not just per-clip URLs and generation IDs.

4. Upgrade the agent invocation contract.
Extend the agent payload and server-side types to accept selected shot context plus richer clip context: `track`, `at`, `duration`, `end`, and pinned-group membership. Preserve backward compatibility for plain clip-only sends.

5. Stop relying on prompt-text inference.
Update server prompt construction and tool logic so “after this clip” and “in the same shot” resolve from structured fields first. Keep timeline summary as fallback/debug context, not the primary source of truth.

6. Implement the duplicate/edit/place workflow.
Add a dedicated operation for “duplicate this, optionally edit it, then place it after the selected clip.” It should handle source resolution, async task creation, result polling, timeline insertion, and same-shot placement rules.

7. Make shot placement semantics explicit.
Define what “same shot” means in both systems: generation DB shot membership and timeline pinned shot-group membership. Add explicit behavior for inserting into a pinned image group vs adding a free clip.

8. Add regression coverage.
Test shot-strip selection behavior, shot payload construction, server context resolution, same-shot insertion, and duplicate/edit/place-after flow. Include edge cases for grouped image shots, video-mode pinned groups, and mixed clip selections.

**Dependencies**
1. Steps 1 to 3 first.
2. Step 4 depends on 3.
3. Steps 5 and 6 depend on 4.
4. Step 7 depends on 6.
5. Step 8 runs across all steps and finishes last.

**Acceptance Criteria**
- Clicking a shot strip selects the whole shot.
- The agent can tell the difference between a clip selection and a shot selection.
- “After this clip” uses exact timeline coordinates, not summary-string matching.
- “In the same shot” inserts into the correct shot context.
- “Make the next image an edit of this image” works end-to-end without manual placement.

If you want, I can turn this into an ordered implementation doc in the repo as the next step.

> AGENT

Root cause was two-layered, and both were real.

In [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py#L93), `megaplan` treated Codex `critique` as a hard timeout even when Codex had already produced valid structured output. Then the timeout payload was being stored as a Python bytes literal like `b'...'`, so the JSON recovery logic could not parse it anyway. I patched both sides: timeout stream decoding in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py#L118) and timeout-time payload salvage in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py#L320) plus the `run_codex_step()` timeout path in [workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/workers.py#L1047). I added regressions in [test_workers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/tests/test_workers.py#L898).

Verification:
- `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m pytest /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/tests/test_workers.py -q`
- Result: `62 passed`
- Reran `megaplan critique --plan implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052`
- Current status: `critiqued`
- Next step: `gate`

So `megaplan` is working again for this plan. The stuck plan is now at the expected state instead of falsely failing at `critique`.

}

> AGENT

Because the UI copy is still using the old clip-count formatter.

Current behavior:
- [useSelectedMediaClips.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSelectedMediaClips.ts#L20) `buildSummary()` only counts `imageCount` and `videoCount`.
- [AgentChat.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.tsx#L155) recomputes the visible composer summary from that same function after merging clips.
- [AgentChatMessage.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.tsx#L27) also formats sent attachments as just `"N images, M videos attached"`.

So even though attachments now carry `shotId` and `shotName`, the chat window never uses them for display. It still sees “4 selected image clips”, not “1 shot (4 images)”.

So the missing piece is purely presentation logic:
- group selected attachments by `shotId`
- render grouped shots as `1 shot (4 images)` or `2 shots (7 images)`
- then append leftover ungrouped media like `and 2 more images`
- apply that in both the composer summary and the sent-message summary

That is why it still shows the old text. The payload got smarter; the label formatter did not.

> AGENT

Fixed.

Cause: [GallerySelectionContext.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/GallerySelectionContext.tsx#L228) was still calling `buildSummary(imageCount, videoCount)` after I changed `buildSummary()` to be shot-aware over clip arrays, so React hit `clips.forEach is not a function`.

What I changed:
- Updated [GallerySelectionContext.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/GallerySelectionContext.tsx) to call `buildSummary(selectedGalleryClips)`.
- Made [useSelectedMediaClips.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSelectedMediaClips.ts) backward-compatible so `buildSummary()` accepts either clip arrays or the old `(imageCount, videoCount)` signature.
- Kept the shot-aware summary behavior for the chat composer and sent-message bubbles.

Verified:
- `npx vitest run src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useAgentSession.test.tsx`
- `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false`

You should be able to reload and use the chat without that crash now.

> AGENT

Implemented.

The attachment strip now groups a fully selected shot into one bordered box and shows the shot’s images together there, with a shot-level `X` plus per-item `X` buttons. Removing either one updates the underlying selection state instead of just hiding the preview, so timeline selection and gallery selection stay in sync. The key changes are in [AgentChatMessage.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.tsx), [AgentChat.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.tsx), and the new targeted selection APIs in [GallerySelectionContext.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/contexts/GallerySelectionContext.tsx), [useTimelineSelection.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineSelection.ts), [useTimelineState.types.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.types.ts), and [useTimelineState.contexts.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.contexts.ts).

Verified with:
- `npx vitest run src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.test.tsx`
- `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false`

I did not run a manual browser smoke test, so the remaining check is just visual confirmation in the actual editor.

I understand the failure modes. I’m checking the actual editor context wiring first, because the `replaceTimelineSelection` crash means the runtime provider path is missing that new op even though the types compiled. After that I’ll patch the layout issues in the attachment strip so the shot box and `X` buttons behave correctly.

> AGENT

I found the runtime cause. `useTimelineEditorContextValue()` was producing `replaceTimelineSelection`, but [VideoEditorProvider.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/contexts/VideoEditorProvider.tsx) never forwarded it into the ops context, so the chat got `undefined` at runtime. I’m patching that, and at the same time I’m restructuring the preview box so the `X` button is no longer inside an `overflow-hidden` container and the shot wrapper shrink-wraps instead of stretching wide.

- forward `replaceTimelineSelection` through the actual editor ops provider,
- make the chat use a safe runtime fallback so this can’t hard-crash again,

> AGENT

Fixed the crash and the layout issues.

The runtime error was real: [VideoEditorProvider.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/contexts/VideoEditorProvider.tsx) wasn’t forwarding `replaceTimelineSelection` into the actual ops context, so [AgentChat.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.tsx) got `undefined` at runtime. I wired that through and also added a safe fallback in the chat so this path won’t hard-crash again.

I also adjusted [AgentChatMessage.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.tsx) so:
- the `X` is no longer clipped by the image bounds,
- shot boxes shrink-wrap instead of stretching wide for a single item,
- removing one item from a shot only changes selection state, so the remaining items can deconstruct back into individual attachments instead of behaving like a still-selected full shot.

Verified with:
- `npx vitest run src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/contexts/VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx`
- `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false`

If you still see the old crash in the browser, reload once so the provider change is picked up.

> AGENT

Removed.

The perf diagnostics are now disabled by default in [perf-diagnostics.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/perf-diagnostics.ts), so those `[PERF] diagnostics active`, `long-task`, `render-storm`, and related console errors will stop showing in normal use.

I kept an explicit opt-in path in case we want them later:
- `localStorage.setItem('reigh:perf-diagnostics', '1')`
- or `window.__REIGH_PERF_DIAGNOSTICS__ = true`

Verified with `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false`.

A reload should clear the spam.

> DEVELOPER

In the chat window, sometimes like URLs and stuff end up very, very long. Like the attach and as a result, the chat window goes, it makes becomes right scrollable. Can you understand why? https://[REDACTED]/rest/v1/rpc/update_timeline_config_versioned

> AGENT

I’m checking the chat bubble styles first. A raw URL causing horizontal scroll usually means one of the message containers is preserving long unbroken tokens without `overflow-wrap`, so I’ll patch the text containers rather than trimming the content.

> AGENT

I found the main culprit: the message body uses `whitespace-pre-wrap` but does not force long tokens to wrap, so a raw URL becomes one unbreakable line and expands the bubble. I’m patching the message and tool-output containers with `min-w-0` plus word-breaking so long URLs can wrap inside the bubble instead of making the whole panel horizontally scroll.

> AGENT

Yes. The reason was exactly that the chat bubble preserved long unbroken strings like raw URLs, so the bubble expanded wider than the panel and the whole chat became horizontally scrollable.

I fixed that in [AgentChatMessage.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.tsx) by:
- adding `min-w-0` to the bubble/tool-row containers,
- making message text use `break-words` and `overflow-wrap:anywhere`,
- making tool command/result text wrap instead of forcing width.

Verified with:
- `npx vitest run src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChat.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/components/AgentChat/AgentChatMessage.test.tsx`
- `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false`

A reload should make those long Supabase URLs wrap normally inside the chat window.
